```
     1→package transcript
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"bufio"
     5→	"bytes"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"io"
     9→	"os"
    10→	"strings"
    11→
    12→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/textutil"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// ParseFromBytes parses transcript content from a byte slice.
    16→// Uses bufio.Reader to handle arbitrarily long lines.
    17→func ParseFromBytes(content []byte) ([]Line, error) {
    18→	var lines []Line
    19→	reader := bufio.NewReader(bytes.NewReader(content))
    20→
    21→	for {
    22→		lineBytes, err := reader.ReadBytes('\n')
    23→		if err != nil && err != io.EOF {
    24→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
    25→		}
    26→
    27→		// Handle empty line or EOF without content
    28→		if len(lineBytes) == 0 {
    29→			if err == io.EOF {
    30→				break
    31→			}
    32→			continue
    33→		}
    34→
    35→		var line Line
    36→		if err := json.Unmarshal(lineBytes, &line); err == nil {
    37→			lines = append(lines, line)
    38→		}
    39→
    40→		if err == io.EOF {
    41→			break
    42→		}
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	return lines, nil
    46→}
    47→
    48→// ParseFromFileAtLine reads and parses a transcript file starting from a specific line.
    49→// Uses bufio.Reader to handle arbitrarily long lines (no size limit).
    50→// Returns:
    51→//   - lines: parsed transcript lines from startLine onwards (malformed lines skipped)
    52→//   - totalLines: total number of lines in the file (including malformed ones)
    53→//   - error: any error encountered during reading
    54→//
    55→// The startLine parameter is 0-indexed (startLine=0 reads from the beginning).
    56→// This is useful for incremental parsing when you've already processed some lines.
    57→func ParseFromFileAtLine(path string, startLine int) ([]Line, int, error) {
    58→	file, err := os.Open(path) //nolint:gosec // path is a controlled transcript file path
    59→	if err != nil {
    60→		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to open transcript: %w", err)
    61→	}
    62→	defer func() { _ = file.Close() }()
    63→
    64→	var lines []Line
    65→	reader := bufio.NewReader(file)
    66→
    67→	totalLines := 0
    68→	for {
    69→		lineBytes, err := reader.ReadBytes('\n')
    70→		if err != nil && err != io.EOF {
    71→			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
    72→		}
    73→
    74→		// Handle empty line or EOF without content
    75→		if len(lineBytes) == 0 {
    76→			if err == io.EOF {
    77→				break
    78→			}
    79→			continue
    80→		}
    81→
    82→		// Count all lines for totalLines, but only parse after startLine
    83→		if totalLines >= startLine {
    84→			var line Line
    85→			if err := json.Unmarshal(lineBytes, &line); err == nil {
    86→				lines = append(lines, line)
    87→			}
    88→		}
    89→		totalLines++
    90→
    91→		if err == io.EOF {
    92→			break
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	return lines, totalLines, nil
    97→}
    98→
    99→// SliceFromLine returns the content starting from line number `startLine` (0-indexed).
   100→// This is used to extract only the checkpoint-specific portion of a cumulative transcript.
   101→// For example, if startLine is 2, lines 0 and 1 are skipped and the result starts at line 2.
   102→// Returns empty slice if startLine exceeds the number of lines.
   103→func SliceFromLine(content []byte, startLine int) []byte {
   104→	if len(content) == 0 || startLine <= 0 {
   105→		return content
   106→	}
   107→
   108→	// Find the byte offset where startLine begins
   109→	lineCount := 0
   110→	offset := 0
   111→	for i, b := range content {
   112→		if b == '\n' {
   113→			lineCount++
   114→			if lineCount == startLine {
   115→				offset = i + 1
   116→				break
   117→			}
   118→		}
   119→	}
   120→
   121→	// If we didn't find enough lines, return empty
   122→	if lineCount < startLine {
   123→		return nil
   124→	}
   125→
   126→	// If offset is beyond content, return empty
   127→	if offset >= len(content) {
   128→		return nil
   129→	}
   130→
   131→	return content[offset:]
   132→}
   133→
   134→// ExtractUserContent extracts user content from a raw message.
   135→// Handles both string and array content formats.
   136→// IDE-injected context tags (like <ide_opened_file>) are stripped from the result.
   137→// Returns empty string if the message cannot be parsed or contains no text.
   138→func ExtractUserContent(message json.RawMessage) string {
   139→	var msg UserMessage
   140→	if err := json.Unmarshal(message, &msg); err != nil {
   141→		return ""
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	// Handle string content
   145→	if str, ok := msg.Content.(string); ok {
   146→		return textutil.StripIDEContextTags(str)
   147→	}
   148→
   149→	// Handle array content (only if it contains text blocks)
   150→	if arr, ok := msg.Content.([]interface{}); ok {
   151→		var texts []string
   152→		for _, item := range arr {
   153→			if m, ok := item.(map[string]interface{}); ok {
   154→				if m["type"] == ContentTypeText {
   155→					if text, ok := m["text"].(string); ok {
   156→						texts = append(texts, text)
   157→					}
   158→				}
   159→			}
   160→		}
   161→		if len(texts) > 0 {
   162→			return textutil.StripIDEContextTags(strings.Join(texts, "\n\n"))
   163→		}
   164→	}
   165→
   166→	return ""
   167→}
   168→
```

```
   266→// ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset extracts files modified since a given line number.
   267→func (c *CursorAgent) ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(path string, startOffset int) (files []string, currentPosition int, err error) {
   268→	if path == "" {
   269→		return nil, 0, nil
   270→	}
   271→
   272→	file, openErr := os.Open(path) //nolint:gosec // Path comes from Cursor transcript location
   273→	if openErr != nil {
   274→		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to open transcript file: %w", openErr)
   275→	}
   276→	defer file.Close()
   277→
   278→	reader := bufio.NewReader(file)
   279→	var lines []transcript.Line
   280→	lineNum := 0
   281→
   282→	for {
   283→		lineData, readErr := reader.ReadBytes('\n')
   284→		if readErr != nil && readErr != io.EOF {
   285→			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", readErr)
   286→		}
   287→
   288→		if len(lineData) > 0 {
   289→			lineNum++
   290→			if lineNum > startOffset {
   291→				var line transcript.Line
   292→				if parseErr := json.Unmarshal(lineData, &line); parseErr == nil {
   293→					lines = append(lines, line)
   294→				}
   295→			}
   296→		}
   297→
   298→		if readErr == io.EOF {
   299→			break
   300→		}
   301→	}
   302→
   303→	return extractModifiedFiles(lines), lineNum, nil
   304→}
   305→
   306→// ChunkTranscript splits a JSONL transcript at line boundaries.
   307→func (c *CursorAgent) ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error) {
   308→	chunks, err := agent.ChunkJSONL(content, maxSize)
   309→	if err != nil {
   310→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to chunk JSONL transcript: %w", err)
   311→	}
   312→	return chunks, nil
   313→}
   314→
   315→// ReassembleTranscript concatenates JSONL chunks with newlines.

   318→}
   319→
   320→// extractModifiedFiles extracts file paths from transcript lines that contain file-modifying tools.
   321→func extractModifiedFiles(lines []transcript.Line) []string {
```

OK so I can see the user has been debugging this. They've added:
1. A `Role` field to `transcript.Line` (Cursor uses `role` instead of `type` for message type)
2. Changed `ExtractPrompts` to check `Role` instead of `Type` 
3. Added `role` fallback in `extractUserPromptsFromLines` for condensation
4. Debug logging in `SaveStep`

Now I understand the picture. The Cursor transcript format uses `role` field (like `"role": "user"`) instead of Claude Code's `type` field (`"type": "user"`). This means:

1. **`extractModifiedFiles`** in `cursor.go:326` checks `lines[i].Type != transcript.TypeAssistant` — but Cursor's lines have `Type` empty and use `Role` instead. So **no assistant lines are ever found**, meaning **no modified files are extracted from the transcript**.

2. If `extractModifiedFiles` returns nothing, and git status also shows no changes (e.g., if the agent's changes are all in the working tree but it's a clean comparison), the checkpoint tree matches the previous one and gets **skipped**.

The root fix: `extractModifiedFiles` needs to check `Role` as well as `Type`.

The issue is that `extractModifiedFiles` (and `ExtractSummary`) filter on `lines[i].Type == "assistant"`, but Cursor transcripts use `"role": "assistant"` instead.

Yep, same problem at line 91: `lines[i].Type != transcript.TypeAssistant`. 

Cursor's JSONL uses `"role": "assistant"` instead of `"type": "assistant"`. So three places in the Cursor agent need to check `Role` instead of (or in addition to) `Type`:

1. **`extractModifiedFiles`** (`cursor.go:326`) — checks `Type` for assistant lines
2. **`ExtractSummary`** (`lifecycle.go:91`) — checks `Type` for assistant lines
3. **`ExtractPrompts`** (`lifecycle.go:67`) — you already fixed this in your unstaged change (`.Role` instead of `.Type`)

The simplest fix: add a helper on `transcript.Line` that returns the effective type/role, or just check both fields in the Cursor agent. Since `transcript.Line` now has both `Type` and `Role`, the Cursor code should check `Role`:

> AGENT

The root cause: Cursor's JSONL transcript uses `"role": "assistant"` / `"role": "user"` while Claude Code uses `"type": "assistant"` / `"type": "user"`. Three places in the Cursor agent were checking `lines[i].Type` instead of `lines[i].Role`:

1. **`extractModifiedFiles`** (`cursor.go:326`) — never found assistant lines, so no modified files were extracted from the transcript. If git status also showed nothing new, the checkpoint tree matched the previous one and was **skipped** (deduplication), so `StepCount` never incremented.
2. **`ExtractSummary`** (`lifecycle.go:91`) — never found assistant lines, so summary was always empty.
3. **`ExtractPrompts`** (`lifecycle.go:67`) — you already had this fix in your working tree.

All three now check both `Role` and `Type` so they work with both Cursor and Claude Code transcript formats.

```
   198→	repo, err := openRepository()
   199→	if err != nil {
   200→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
   201→	}
   202→
   203→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()

   206→	}
   207→
   208→	status, err := worktree.Status()
   209→	if err != nil {
   210→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get status: %w", err)
   211→	}
   212→
   213→	// Build set of pre-existing untracked files for quick lookup
   214→	var preExisting map[string]bool
   215→	if previouslyUntracked != nil {
   216→		preExisting = make(map[string]bool, len(previouslyUntracked))
   217→		for _, f := range previouslyUntracked {
   218→			preExisting[f] = true
   219→		}
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	var changes FileChanges
   223→	for file, st := range status {
   224→		if paths.IsInfrastructurePath(file) {
   225→			continue
   226→		}
   227→
   228→		switch {
   229→		case st.Worktree == git.Untracked:
   230→			if preExisting != nil {
   231→				if !preExisting[file] {
   232→					changes.New = append(changes.New, file)
   233→				}
   234→			} else {
   235→				changes.New = append(changes.New, file)
   236→			}
   237→		case st.Worktree == git.Deleted || st.Staging == git.Deleted:
   238→			changes.Deleted = append(changes.Deleted, file)
   239→		case st.Worktree == git.Modified || st.Staging == git.Modified ||
   240→			st.Worktree == git.Added || st.Staging == git.Added:
   241→			changes.Modified = append(changes.Modified, file)
   242→		}
   243→	}
   244→
   245→	return &changes, nil
   246→}

   248→// filterToUncommittedFiles removes files from the list that are already committed to HEAD
   249→// with matching content. This prevents re-adding files that an agent committed mid-turn
   250→// (already condensed by PostCommit) back to FilesTouched via SaveStep. Files not in
   251→// HEAD or with different content in the working tree are kept. Fails open: if any git
   252→// operation errors, returns the original list unchanged.
   253→func filterToUncommittedFiles(files []string, repoRoot string) []string {
   254→	if len(files) == 0 {
   255→		return files
   256→	}
   257→
   258→	repo, err := openRepository()
   259→	if err != nil {
   260→		return files // fail open
   261→	}
   262→
   263→	head, err := repo.Head()
   264→	if err != nil {
   265→		return files // fail open (empty repo, detached HEAD, etc.)
   266→	}
   267→
   268→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   269→	if err != nil {
   270→		return files // fail open
   271→	}
   272→
   273→	headTree, err := commit.Tree()
   274→	if err != nil {
   275→		return files // fail open
```

OK, so post-commit is a **git hook** installed by `entire enable`, not a Cursor agent hook. So it fires on `git commit` regardless of the agent. This means if Cursor does `git commit`, the post-commit hook fires, condensation runs, and `StepCount` is read at that point.

But the normal flow is: Cursor agent works → `stop` hook fires → `handleLifecycleTurnEnd` → `SaveStep` (increments StepCount) → user later does `git commit` → post-commit hook → condensation reads StepCount.

If `SaveStep` is being skipped (checkpoint dedup), `StepCount` stays 0. The dedup happens when no files changed. Let me check if Cursor is actually making changes when the user tests.

Actually, let me just ask the user what they're seeing.

The `checkpoint_count` in `metadata.json` comes from `state.StepCount` at condensation time. The fix I made should help `extractModifiedFiles` find files from the transcript. But there are two possible remaining issues:

1. **Did you rebuild before testing?** The changes are all unstaged — if you didn't `go install` or rebuild, the running binary wouldn't have the `Role` fix.

2. **Is the checkpoint being skipped due to tree dedup?** Even with the `Role` fix, if Cursor commits files before the `stop` hook fires, git status would be clean AND `filterToUncommittedFiles` would strip the transcript-extracted files, leaving nothing to checkpoint. The tree would match the parent → dedup → `StepCount` stays 0.

```
     1→// hook_registry.go provides hook command registration for agents.
     2→// The lifecycle dispatcher (DispatchLifecycleEvent) handles all lifecycle events.
     3→// PostTodo is the only hook that's handled directly (not via lifecycle dispatcher).
     4→package cli
     5→
     6→import (
     7→	"context"
     8→	"errors"
     9→	"fmt"
    10→	"log/slog"
    11→	"time"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    14→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode"
    15→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/cursor"
    16→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli"
    17→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/logging"
    18→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    19→
    20→	"github.com/spf13/cobra"
    21→)
    22→
    23→// agentHookLogCleanup stores the cleanup function for agent hook logging.
    24→// Set by PersistentPreRunE, called by PersistentPostRunE.
    25→var agentHookLogCleanup func()
    26→
    27→// currentHookAgentName stores the agent name for the currently executing hook.
    28→// Set by newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging before calling the handler.
    29→// This allows handlers to know which agent invoked the hook without guessing.
    30→var currentHookAgentName agent.AgentName
    31→
    32→// GetCurrentHookAgent returns the agent for the currently executing hook.
    33→// Returns the agent based on the hook command structure (e.g., "entire hooks claude-code ...")
    34→// rather than guessing from directory presence.
    35→// Falls back to GetAgent() if not in a hook context.
    36→func GetCurrentHookAgent() (agent.Agent, error) {
    37→	if currentHookAgentName == "" {
    38→		return nil, errors.New("not in a hook context: agent name not set")
    39→	}
    40→
    41→	ag, err := agent.Get(currentHookAgentName)
    42→	if err != nil {
    43→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("getting hook agent %q: %w", currentHookAgentName, err)
    44→	}
    45→	return ag, nil
    46→}
    47→
    48→// newAgentHooksCmd creates a hooks subcommand for an agent that implements HookHandler.
    49→// It dynamically creates subcommands for each hook the agent supports.
    50→func newAgentHooksCmd(agentName agent.AgentName, handler agent.HookHandler) *cobra.Command {
    51→	cmd := &cobra.Command{
    52→		Use:    string(agentName),
    53→		Short:  handler.Description() + " hook handlers",
    54→		Hidden: true,
    55→		PersistentPreRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    56→			agentHookLogCleanup = initHookLogging()
    57→			return nil
    58→		},
    59→		PersistentPostRunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    60→			if agentHookLogCleanup != nil {
    61→				agentHookLogCleanup()
    62→			}
    63→			return nil
    64→		},
    65→	}
    66→
    67→	for _, hookName := range handler.GetHookNames() {
    68→		cmd.AddCommand(newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging(agentName, hookName))
    69→	}
    70→
    71→	return cmd
    72→}
    73→
    74→// getHookType returns the hook type based on the hook name.
    75→// Returns "subagent" for task-related hooks (pre-task, post-task, post-todo),
    76→// "tool" for tool-related hooks (before-tool, after-tool),
    77→// "agent" for all other agent hooks.
    78→func getHookType(hookName string) string {
    79→	// Note: Claude Code and Cursor share the same hook names (pre-task, post-task, post-todo)
    80→	switch hookName {
    81→	case claudecode.HookNamePreTask, claudecode.HookNamePostTask, claudecode.HookNamePostTodo:
    82→		return "subagent"
    83→	case geminicli.HookNameBeforeTool, geminicli.HookNameAfterTool:
    84→		return "tool"
    85→	default:
    86→		return "agent"
    87→	}
    88→}
    89→
    90→// newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging creates a command for a specific hook verb with structured logging.
    91→// It uses the lifecycle dispatcher (ParseHookEvent → DispatchLifecycleEvent) as the primary path.
    92→// PostTodo is handled directly as it's Claude-specific and not part of the lifecycle dispatcher.
    93→func newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging(agentName agent.AgentName, hookName string) *cobra.Command {
    94→	return &cobra.Command{
    95→		Use:    hookName,
    96→		Hidden: true,
    97→		Short:  "Called on " + hookName,
    98→		RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
    99→			// Skip silently if not in a git repository - hooks shouldn't prevent the agent from working
   100→			if _, err := paths.RepoRoot(); err != nil {
   101→				return nil
   102→			}
   103→
   104→			// Skip if Entire is not enabled
   105→			enabled, err := IsEnabled()
   106→			if err == nil && !enabled {
   107→				return nil
   108→			}
   109→
   110→			start := time.Now()
   111→
   112→			// Initialize logging context with agent name
   113→			ctx := logging.WithAgent(logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "hooks"), agentName)
   114→
   115→			// Get strategy name for logging
   116→			strategyName := GetStrategy().Name()
   117→
   118→			hookType := getHookType(hookName)
   119→
   120→			logging.Debug(ctx, "hook invoked",
   121→				slog.String("hook", hookName),
   122→				slog.String("hook_type", hookType),
   123→				slog.String("strategy", strategyName),
   124→			)
   125→
   126→			// Set the current hook agent so handlers can retrieve it
   127→			currentHookAgentName = agentName
   128→			defer func() { currentHookAgentName = "" }()
   129→
   130→			// Use the lifecycle dispatcher for all hooks
   131→			var hookErr error
   132→			ag, agentErr := agent.Get(agentName)
   133→			if agentErr != nil {
```

> AGENT

So all three lists are empty:

1. `relModifiedFiles` — from transcript extraction, then filtered by `filterToUncommittedFiles`
2. `relNewFiles` — from `DetectFileChanges` (git status)
3. `relDeletedFiles` — from `DetectFileChanges` (git status)

The `Role` fix should help transcript extraction, but `filterToUncommittedFiles` strips files that are already committed to HEAD. If Cursor committed the files before the `stop` hook fires, the transcript would find files but they'd all get filtered out.

And `DetectFileChanges` (git status) would also show clean since everything was committed.

The problem is this early return assumes "no changes = nothing to save." But even with no uncommitted file changes, there's still valuable session metadata (transcript, prompts, summary) worth checkpointing. The `SaveStep` → `WriteTemporary` dedup already handles the case where nothing changed — it should be `SaveStep`'s decision to skip, not this early return.
